, and there exists a longer prefix
v
such that
u\leq v
and

v{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}
be the participant with the maximum
|\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}|
in
\mathcal{P}
.
Let
u^{\prime}=\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}))
.
Clearly,
u^{\prime}\leq w^{\prime}
.
Because
u^{\prime}
is
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}))
for the participant with the longest
\rho^{\prime}_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}
, it holds for all participants
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}
that

u{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leq u^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}
.
Then, there must exist
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{*}
such that

u{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\cdot y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}=u^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
be defined in this way for each participant.
We construct
v\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
such that
uv\sim u^{\prime}
.
Let
v
be initialized with
\varepsilon
.
If there exists some participant in
\mathcal{P}
such that
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}[0]\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},!}
, append
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
to
v
and update
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
.
If not, for all participants
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}[0]\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},?}
.
Each symbol
y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}[0]
for all participants appears in
u^{\prime}
.
Let
i_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
denote for each participant the index in
u^{\prime}
such that
u^{\prime}[i]=y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}[0]
.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
be the participant with the minimum index
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
.
Append
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
to
v
and update
y_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
.
Termination is guaranteed by the strictly decreasing measure of
\sum_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}|y_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}|
.

We argue that
uv
satisfies the inductive invariant of channel compliancy.
In the case where
v
is extended with a send action, channel compliancy is trivially re-established.
In the receive case, channel compliancy is re-established by the fact that the append order for receive actions follows that in
u^{\prime}
, which is channel-compliant by construction.
We conclude that
uv\sim u^{\prime}
by applying
(
Majumdar et al., 2021a
, Lemma 22)
.

∎
See
4.12

Proof.

We prove the claim by induction on the length of
w
.

Base Case.

w=\varepsilon
.
The trace
w=\varepsilon
is trivially consistent with all maximal runs, and
I(w)
therefore contains all maximal runs.
By assumption,
\mathcal{S}
contains at least one maximal run.
Thus,
I(w)
is non-empty.

Induction Step.

Let
wx
be an extension of
w
by
x\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}
.

The induction hypothesis states that

I(w)\neq\emptyset
.
To re-establish the induction hypothesis, we need to show

I(wx)\neq\emptyset
.
We proceed by case analysis on whether
x
is a receive or send event.

Send Case.

Let
x
=
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
.
By
Lemma

4.14
, there exists a run in
I(wx)
that shares a prefix with a run in
I(w)
.
I(wx)\neq\emptyset
again follows immediately.

Receive Case.

Let
x
=
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?m
.
By
Lemma

4.13
,
I(wx)=I(w)
.
I(wx)\neq\emptyset
follows trivially from the induction hypothesis and this equality.
∎

Proposition A.1 (CLTS traces are channel-compliant).

Let
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
be a CLTS, and let
w\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
be a trace of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
Let
(\vec{s},\xi)
be the
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configuration reached on
w
.
Then,
w
is channel-compliant, and for every pair of participants
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!\hbox{-}})=\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?\hbox{-}})\cdot\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}})
.

The proof of the same proposition for communicating state machines can be generalized directly to CLTSs, and thus we refer the reader to
(
Majumdar et al., 2021a
, Lemma 19)
.
See
4.13

Proof.

Let
x
=
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?m
.
Because
wx
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, there exists a run

(\vec{s}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{w}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s},\xi)\xrightarrow{x}(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

such that

m
is at the head of
\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.

We assume that
I(w)
is non-empty; if
I(w)
is empty then
I(wx)
is trivially empty.

To show
I(w)=I(wx)
, let
\rho\in I(w)
and we show that
\rho\in I(wx)
.
Recall that

I(wx)
is defined as
\bigcap_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\in\mathcal{P}}\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}(wx)
.

Because

\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}(wx)=\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}(w)

for every
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\in\mathcal{P}
with
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
,
it suffices to show that

\rho\in\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(wx)

to show
\rho\in I(wx)
.

We proceed via proof by contradiction so let

\rho\notin\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(wx)
for
\rho\in I(w)
.

Let
\alpha\cdot s_{\mathit{pre}}\xrightarrow{l}s_{\mathit{post}}\cdot\beta
be the unique splitting of
\rho
for
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
matching
w
.
By definition of unique splittings,
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is the active participant in
l
.
Because

\rho\notin\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(wx)
, it follows that

l\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\!:\!m
.
By
Lemma

4.7
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is the receiver in
l
, and
l
is of the form
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\!:\!m^{\prime}
,
where
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
or
m^{\prime}\neq m
.

Before performing case analysis, we first establish a claim that is used in both cases.
Let
\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
denote the largest prefix of
\rho
that is consistent with
w
for
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
.
Formally,

\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}=\max\{\rho\mid\rho\leq\rho~\land~\bigl(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho))\bigr){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leq w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\}
.
Let
\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
be defined analogously.
It is clear that

\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}=\alpha\cdot s_{pre}
.

Claim I.

\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}>\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
.

From
Proposition

A.1
,

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\hbox{-}})=\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\hbox{-}})\cdot\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.
Because
\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}=\alpha\cdot s_{pre}
, it follows that

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\hbox{-}})=\mathcal{V}(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})){\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\hbox{-}})
.

Because
m
is at the head of
\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
by assumption, there exists
u\in\mathcal{V}^{*}
such that

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\hbox{-}})=\mathcal{V}(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}))){\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\hbox{-}}\cdot m\cdot u
.
Thus,

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\hbox{-}})>\mathcal{V}(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}))){\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\hbox{-}}
and

\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}>\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
follows.

End Proof of Claim I.

Case:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
and
m^{\prime}\neq m
.

We discharge this case by showing a contradiction to the assumption that
m
is at the head of
\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.
Because
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\leq\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
and
\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}<\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
from Claim I, it must be the case that

\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{l}s_{post}\leq\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}

and

{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!m^{\prime}
is in
w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}
.
From
Proposition

A.1
, it follows that

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\hbox{-}})=\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\hbox{-}})\cdot m^{\prime}\cdot u^{\prime}
and

\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})=m^{\prime}\cdot u^{\prime}
, i.e.
m^{\prime}
is at the head of
\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.
We find a contradiction to the assumption that
m^{\prime}\neq m
.

Case:

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
.

We discharge this case by showing a contradiction to RC.
First, we establish the existence of a transition
s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m}s_{2}\in T
such that
s_{1}\neq s_{pre}
and
s_{1}
is reachable by
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
on
\texttt{split}^{-1}(w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
.

By the assumption that
wx
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
,
it follows that
wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is a prefix of
\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.
By the canonicity of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, it holds that

\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))\subseteq\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
, and thus

wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
.
Thus, there exists a maximal run
\rho^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{S}
such that

wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}

and

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m}s_{2}\in\rho^{\prime}
.
Because
\mathcal{S}
is sender-driven, there does not exist a state
s\in S
with two outgoing transition labels with different senders.
Therefore,
s_{1}\neq s_{pre}
.

By the fact that

\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{l}s_{post}\cdot\beta
is the unique splitting of
\rho
for
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
matching
w
, it holds that
s_{pre}
is also reachable by
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
on
\texttt{split}^{-1}(w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
.

We instantiate RC with

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m}s_{2}
,
s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m}s_{post}
and
\texttt{split}^{-1}(w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})

to obtain:

\neg(\exists v\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}_{s_{post}}).~v{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=\varepsilon\land\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\_})=\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\_})\cdot m)\kern 5.0pt.

We show, on the contrary, that

\exists v\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}_{s_{post}}).~v{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=\varepsilon\land\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\_})=\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\_})\cdot m\kern 5.0pt.

It is clear that
s_{post}\cdot\beta
is a maximal run in
\mathcal{S}_{s_{post}}
.
By
Lemma

4.15
, to show that a witness
v\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}_{s_{post}})
, it suffices to show that
v
is channel-compliant and furthermore, that for all participants
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}\in\mathcal{P}
,

v{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(s_{post}\cdot\beta)){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}}
.

Recall that
w
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
and is thus channel-compliant.
Intuitively, we obtain a witness for
v
by deleting from
w
symbols that belong to
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{l}s_{post}))
.
Formally, let
v
be initialized to
w
and let
l_{1}\ldots l_{n}=\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{l}s_{post})
.
For each
i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, let
l_{i}\coloneq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}\!:\!m_{i}
.
We check whether
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}!m_{i}\leq w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}}}
, and if so, we delete the symbol
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}!m_{i}
from
w
.
We then check whether
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}?m_{i}\leq w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}}}
, and again delete the symbol if so.
Note that due to the channel-compliancy of
v
, either both symbols are deleted, or only the send action is deleted.
We argue that the inductive invariant of channel-compliancy is satisfied: if a matching pair of send and receive actions are found in
v
and deleted, each of
\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}?\_})
and
\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}!\_})
lose their head message, and
\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}?\_})\leq\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}!\_})
continues to hold; if only the send action is found and deleted, then it must be the case that
\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}_{i}?\_})=\varepsilon
and the invariant is trivially re-established.
Thus, we establish that upon termination,
v
is channel-compliant.
Furthermore, it holds that
s_{post}\cdot\beta\in I^{\mathcal{S}_{s_{post}}}(v)
.

Recall that

\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\_})=\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\_})\cdot m
.
It remains to show that

\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!\_})=\mathcal{V}(v{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?\_})\cdot m
.
This holds from the fact that
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}=\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}<\rho_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
, which means that any labels of the form
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\!:\!\hbox{-}
in
l_{1}\ldots l_{n}
must find and delete a matching pair of send and receive actions in
v
, thus preserving the above equality.

∎

See
4.14

Proof.

Let
x
=
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
.
We prove the claim by induction on the length of
w
.

Base Case.

w=\varepsilon
.
By definition,
I(\varepsilon)
contains all maximal runs in
\mathcal{S}
.
Then,
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}=\varepsilon
and it holds that

s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{pre}
.
We argue that there exists
s_{1}\in S
such that
s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1}
.
From the canonicity of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
and the fact that
x\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))
, it follows that

x\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
.
Thus, there exists
w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})
such that
x\leq w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, and consequently there exists a run
\rho^{\prime}
such that
x\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
The unique splitting of
\rho^{\prime}
for
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
with respect to
\varepsilon
gives us a candidate for
s_{1}
.
By
Definition

4.1
, there exists a
s_{2}
such that
s_{1}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{l}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{2}
.
By the assumption that every run in
\mathcal{S}
extends to a maximal run, there exists a maximal run in
I(x)
.

Induction Step.

Let
wx
be an extension of
w
by
x\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},!}
.
To re-establish the induction hypothesis, we need to show the existence of a run
\bar{\rho}
in
I(wx)
such that

\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\leq\bar{\rho}
.
Since
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is the active participant in
x
, it holds for any
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
that

\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}(w)=\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}(wx)
.
Therefore, to prove the existential claim, it suffices to construct a run
\bar{\rho}
that satisfies:

(1)

\bar{\rho}\in\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(wx)
,

(2)

\bar{\rho}\in I(w)
, and

(3)

\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\leq\bar{\rho}
.

In the case that
l{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=x
, we
are done:
Property
3
and
2
hold by construction, and Property
1
holds by the definition of possible run sets.

In the case that
l{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq x
, we show the existence of a different continuation such that the resulting run satisfies all three conditions.

First, we establish that
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is the sender in
l
.
By definition of unique splitting, we know that
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is active in
l
.
Assume towards a contradiction that
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is the receiver in
l
.
Then,
l
is of the form
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\!:\!m
.
Because
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m}s_{post}\cdot\beta
is a maximal run in
\mathcal{S}
,
we have that

(w\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?m){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
.
By the canonicity of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
,
it holds that

\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})\subseteq\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))
,
and therefore

(w\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?m){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))
.
By assumption that
wx
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, it holds that

(wx){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))
.
From the fact that
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}?m\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},?}
and
x\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},!}
, we find a contradiction to
Lemma

4.7
.
Therefore,
l
must be of the form
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}^{\prime}\!:\!m^{\prime}
, with
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}^{\prime}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
or
m^{\prime}\neq m
.

By assumption that
wx
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, it holds that
wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))
.
By the canonicity of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
(
Definition

4.6
(ii)),
we have

\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}))\subseteq\operatorname{pref}((\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}))

and hence,

wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}})
.
Thus, there exists
v\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})
such that

wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq v{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, and consequently there exists a run
\rho^{\prime}
such that
wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
The unique splitting of
\rho^{\prime}
for
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
with respect to
w
gives us a transition

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2}\in T
.

If
s_{1}=s_{pre}
, then
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2}
is a run in
\mathcal{S}
.
Otherwise, we instantiate SC (
Definition

4.1
) with
s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2}
,
s_{pre}
and the witness
w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
Then, there exists
s^{\prime}
such that

s_{pre}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s^{\prime}
.
We argue that, in fact,

s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}\in T
.
This follows from the fact established above that
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is the sender in
l
, and that
s_{pre}\xrightarrow{l}s_{post}\in T
.
By the assumption that
\mathcal{S}
is sender driven, there does not exist a state with outgoing transitions that do not share a sender.
Therefore,

\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}
is a run in
\mathcal{S}
.

Either way, we have found a run that thus far satisfies Property
1
and
3
regardless of its choice of maximal suffix.
Let
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\bar{s^{\prime}}
be a run in
\mathcal{S}
.
Then, for all choices of
\bar{\beta}
such that
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\bar{s^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}

is a maximal run,
both

wx{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\bar{s^{\prime}}))

and
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\leq\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\bar{s^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}
hold.

Property
2
, however, requires that the projection of
w
onto each participant is consistent with
\bar{\rho}
, and this cannot be ensured by the prefix alone.

We construct the remainder of
\bar{\rho}
by picking an arbitrary maximal suffix to form a candidate run, and iteratively performing suffix replacements on the candidate run until it lands in
I(w)
.
Let
\bar{\beta}
be a run suffix such that

\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\bar{s^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta}

is a maximal run in
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
\rho_{c}
denote this candidate run.

If
\rho_{c}\in I(w)
, we are done.
Otherwise,
\rho_{c}\notin I(w)
and there exists a non-empty set of processes
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
such that for each
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\in\mathcal{Q}
,

(1)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\nleq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

By the fact that
\rho\in I(w)
,

(2)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho)){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

We can rewrite (
1
) and (
2
) above to make explicit their shared prefix
\alpha\cdot s_{pre}
:

(3)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\nleq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\bar{s^{\prime}}\cdot\bar{\beta})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

(4)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}^{\prime}:m^{\prime}}s_{post}\cdot\beta)){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}\kern 5.0pt.

We can further rewrite (
3
) and (
4
) to make explicit their point of disagreement:

(5)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

\displaystyle\nleq(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})).~{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m.~{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m.~\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\bar{\beta}))){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

(6)

\displaystyle w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

\displaystyle\leq(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})).~{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}^{\prime}!m^{\prime}.~{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}^{\prime}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m^{\prime}.~\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\beta))){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}

It is clear that in order for both
5
and
6
to hold, it must be the case that

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}<w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.

We formalize the point of disagreement between
w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
and
\rho_{c}
using an index
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
representing the position of the first disagreeing symbol in
\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})
:

i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\coloneq\text{max}\{i\mid\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})[0..i-1]){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\leq w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\}\kern 5.0pt.

By the maximality of
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
, it holds that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
is the active participant in
\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})[i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}]
.
By the fact that

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}<w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
we know that

i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}>\lvert\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s_{pre})\rvert\kern 5.0pt.

We identify the participant in
\mathcal{Q}
with the
earliest disagreement
in
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c}))
: let
\bar{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
be the participant in
\mathcal{Q}
with the smallest
i_{\bar{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
If two participants that share the same smallest index, then by the fact that both participants are active in
\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})[i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
, it must be the case that one is the sender and one is the receiver: we pick the sender to be
\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
.
Let
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
denote
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c}[i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}])){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.

Claim I

y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is a send event.

Assume by contradiction that
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
is a receive event.
We identify the symbol in
w
that disagrees with
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
: let
w^{\prime}
be the largest prefix of
w
such that
w^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
By definition,
w^{\prime}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}=\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}-1]){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
Let
z
be the next symbol following
w^{\prime}
in
w
; then
w^{\prime}z\leq w
and
z\in Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
with
z\neq y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
.
Furthermore, by No Mixed Choice (
4.7
) we have that
z\in Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}},?}
.

By assumption,

w^{\prime}z\nleq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho_{c})[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}])
.
Therefore, any run with a trace that begins with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
cannot be contained in
\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}(w^{\prime}z)
, or consequently in
I(w^{\prime}z)
.
We show however, that
I(w^{\prime}z)
must contain some runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
.
From Lemma
4.13
for traces
w^{\prime}
and
w^{\prime}z
, we obtain that

I(w^{\prime})=I(w^{\prime}z)
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that
I(w^{\prime})
contains runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
.

Claim II

\forall w^{\prime\prime}\leq w^{\prime}.\,I(w^{\prime\prime})
contains runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
.

We prove the claim via induction on
w^{\prime}
.

The base case is trivial from the fact that
I(\varepsilon)
contains all maximal runs.

For the inductive step, let
w^{\prime\prime}y\leq w^{\prime}
.

In the case that
y\in Σ_{?}
, we know
I(w^{\prime\prime}y)=I(w^{\prime\prime})
from Lemma
4.13
and the witness from
I(w^{\prime\prime})
can be reused.

In the case that
y\in Σ_{!}
, let
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}
be the active participant of
y
and let
\rho^{\prime}
be a run in
I(w^{\prime\prime})
beginning with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
given by the inner induction hypothesis.
Let
\alpha^{\prime}\cdot s_{3}\xrightarrow{l^{\prime}}s_{4}\cdot\beta^{\prime}
be the unique splitting of
\rho^{\prime}
for
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}
with respect to
w^{\prime\prime}
.
If
\texttt{split}(l^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}=y
, then
\rho^{\prime}
can be used as the witness.
Otherwise,
\texttt{split}(l^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}\neq y
, and
\rho^{\prime}\notin\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}}(w^{\prime\prime}y)
.

The outer induction hypothesis holds for all prefixes of
w
: we instantiate it with
w^{\prime\prime}
and
y
to obtain:

\exists~\rho^{\prime\prime}\in I(w^{\prime\prime}y).~\alpha^{\prime}\cdot s_{3}\leq\rho^{\prime\prime}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}
be defined as before; it follows that
\rho^{\prime}[i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}]=s_{3}
.
It must be the case that
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}>i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
: if
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}\leq i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
, because
\rho_{c}
and
\rho^{\prime}
share a prefix
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
and
w^{\prime\prime}y\leq w
,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}
would be the earliest disagreeing participant instead of
\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
.

Because
i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}>i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
,

\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]=\rho^{\prime}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]\leq\rho^{\prime}[0..i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}]
.
Because
\rho^{\prime}[0..i_{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{s}}}]=\alpha^{\prime}\cdot s_{3}\leq\rho^{\prime\prime}
,
it follows from prefix transitivity that
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]\leq\rho^{\prime\prime}
,
thus re-establishing the induction hypothesis for
w^{\prime\prime}y
with
\rho^{\prime\prime}
as a witness run that begins with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
.

This concludes our proof that
I(w^{\prime})
contains runs that begin with
\rho_{c}[0..i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
, and in turn our proof by contradiction that
y_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
must be a send event.

Having established that
l_{i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
is a send event for
\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
, we can now reason from the canonicity of
\{\!\!\{T_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
and SC and conclude that there exists an outgoing transition from
\rho_{c}[i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}]
and a maximal suffix such that the resulting run no longer disagrees with
w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
The reasoning is identical to that which is used to construct our candidate run
\rho_{c}
, and is thus omitted.
We update our candidate run
\rho_{c}
with the correct transition label and maximal suffix, update the set of states
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{P}
to the new set of participants that disagree with the new candidate run, and repeat the construction above on the new candidate run until
\mathcal{Q}
is empty.

Termination is guaranteed in at most
|w|
steps by the fact that the number of symbols in
w
that agree with the candidate run up to
i_{\bar{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}}
must increase.

Upon termination, the resulting
\rho_{c}
serves as our witness for
\bar{\rho}
and
\bar{\rho}
thus satisfies the final remaining property
3
:
\bar{\rho}\in I(w)
.
This concludes our proof by induction of the prefix-preservation of send transitions.
∎
See
4.18

Proof.

Let communicating LTS
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implement
\mathcal{S}
.
Specifically, we contradict protocol fidelity, and show that
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})\neq\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}})
by constructing a witness
v_{0}
satisfying:

(1)

v_{0}
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, and

(2)

I(v_{0})=\emptyset
.

The reasoning for the sufficiency of the above two conditions is as follows.
To prove the inequality of the two languages, it suffices to prove the inequality of their respective prefix sets, i.e.

\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}))\neq\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}))\kern 5.0pt.

Specifically, we show the existence of a
v\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
such that

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\in\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}})\}~\land

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\notin\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})\}\kern 5.0pt.

Because
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is deadlock-free by assumption, every trace can be extended to a maximal trace.
Therefore, every trace
v\in Σ_{\mathit{async}}^{*}
of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
is a member of the prefix set of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
, i.e.

\exists~(\vec{s},\xi).~(\vec{s}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{v}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s},\xi)\implies v\in\{u\mid u\leq w\land w\in\mathcal{L}(\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}})\}\kern 5.0pt.

For any
w\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})
, it holds that
I(w)\neq\emptyset
.

Because
I(\hbox{-})
is monotonically decreasing, if
I(w)
is non-empty then for any
v\leq w
,
I(v)
is non-empty.
By the following, to show that a word
v
is not a member of the prefix set of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})
it suffices to show that
I(v)
is empty:

\forall v\in Σ^{*}.~I(v)=\emptyset\implies\forall w.~v\leq w\implies w\notin\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})\kern 5.0pt.

Send Coherence.

Assume that SC does not hold for some transition

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2}\in T
.
The negation of SC says that there exists a simultaneously reachable state with no post-state reachable on
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\!\to\!{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\!:\!m
.
Formally, let
s\in S
be a state with
s\neq s_{1}
and
u\in Σ^{*}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
be a word such that
s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1},s
. Then, there does not exist
s^{\prime}\in S
such that
s\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s^{\prime}
.

Because
s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s
, there exists a run
\alpha\cdot s
such that
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s)){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=u
.

Let
\bar{w}
be
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))
.
Let
\bar{w}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
be our witness
v_{0}
; we show that
v_{0}
satisfies
1
and
2
.

Because
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathcal{S}
,
\bar{w}
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
and there exists a configuration
(\vec{t},\xi)
of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
such that

(\vec{t}_{0},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\bar{w}}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{t},\xi)
.
Because
s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1}
, there again exists a run
\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{1}
such that
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{1})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=u
.
Thus,
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2}))
is a prefix of
\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})
and consequently,

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is a prefix of
\mathcal{L}(B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.
In other words,
u\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
is a prefix of
\mathcal{L}(B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}})
.
Because
B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
is deterministic, there exists an outgoing transition from
\vec{s}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
labeled with
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
.
Because send transitions are always enabled in a communicating LTS,

\bar{w}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
Thus,
1
is established for
v_{0}
.

It remains to show that
v_{0}
satisfies
2
, namely
I(\bar{w}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m)=\emptyset
.

Claim.
All runs in
I(\bar{w})
begin with
\alpha\cdot s
.

Proof of Claim.

This claim follows from the fact that
\mathcal{S}
is deterministic and sender-driven.
Assume by contradiction that
\rho^{\prime}\in I(\bar{w})
and
\rho^{\prime}
does not begin with
\alpha\cdot s
.
Because
\alpha\cdot s\neq\rho^{\prime}
, and
\mathcal{S}
is deterministic,

\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s)\neq\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})
.
Let
l=\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s)
and let
l^{\prime}=\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})
.
Moreover, let
\bar{l}
be the largest common prefix of
l
and
l^{\prime}
.
From the assumption that
\mathcal{S}
is sender-driven, the first divergence between the traces of any two runs must correspond to a send action by some participant.
Let
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}
be the sender in the first divergence between
l
and
l^{\prime}
.
Because
\rho^{\prime}\in\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}(\bar{w})
, it holds that

\bar{w}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\rho^{\prime})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}
.
We can rewrite the inequality as

\texttt{split}(l){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}\leq\texttt{split}(l^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}
.

Because
\bar{l}
is the largest common prefix shared by
l
and
l^{\prime}
,
\texttt{split}(l){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}
and
\texttt{split}(l^{\prime}){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}
are respectively of the form

\bar{l}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}!m^{\prime}_{i}\cdot z^{\prime}
and

\bar{l}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{j}!m^{\prime}_{j}\cdot y^{\prime}
, with

{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{j}
or
m^{\prime}_{i}\neq m^{\prime}_{j}
.
From this and

\bar{l}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{i}!m^{\prime}_{i}\cdot z^{\prime}\leq\bar{l}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}}}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}^{\prime}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}_{j}!m^{\prime}_{j}\cdot y^{\prime}
, we arrive at a contradiction.

End Proof of Claim.

Because
I(\hbox{-})
is monotonically decreasing,
I(v_{0})\subseteq I(\bar{w})
.
With
Claim
, every run in
I(v_{0})
begins with
\alpha\cdot s
.
From the negation of SC, there does not exist
s^{\prime}\in S
such that
s\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s^{\prime}
, and thus there does not exist a maximal run
\bar{\rho}\in\mathcal{S}
such that

v_{0}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}\leq\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\bar{\rho})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

Therefore,

\operatorname{R}^{\mathcal{S}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}(\bar{w}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m)=\emptyset
, and

I(\bar{w}\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m)=\emptyset

follows.

This concludes our proof by contradiction for the necessity of SC.

Receive Coherence.

Assume that RC does not hold for a pair of transitions

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2},s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}\in T
.

Then,
s\neq s_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
and let
u\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{*}
be a word such that

s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1},s
.
Furthermore there exists

w\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}_{s^{\prime}}))
with

w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}=\varepsilon\land\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!\_})=\mathcal{V}(w{\Downarrow}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?\_})\cdot m
.

Because

s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1},s
and

s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}
,
there exists a run
\alpha\cdot s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}
such that

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s)){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}=u
.

Let
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
be our witness
v_{0}
; we show that
v_{0}
satisfies
1
and
2
.

First, we show that
v_{0}
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
.
We reason about each extension of
v_{0}
in turn, starting with
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))
.
It is clear that
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
: this follows immediately from the assumption that
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathcal{S}
.
Let
(\vec{s},\xi)
be the
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configuration reached on
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))
:

(\vec{s_{0}},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s},\xi)

Next, we reason about the extension
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w
together.
We first establish that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}_{s}))
.
Because
w\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}_{s^{\prime}}))
, there exists a maximal run
s^{\prime}\cdot\beta
such that
s^{\prime}\cdot\beta\in I(w)
.
Observe that
s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}\cdot\beta\in I({\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w)
and that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w
remains channel-compliant due to the assumption that
w{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}=\varepsilon
.
Thus, by
Lemma

4.15
it holds that
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}_{s}))
.
Therefore,
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S}))
, and by the assumption that
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathcal{S}
,

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w
is a trace of
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
:

(\vec{s_{0}},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s},\xi)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w}(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})

Finally, we reason about the extension
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
.
We show that there exists a
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
configuration
(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime\prime})
such that

(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m}(\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime})
.
To do so, we need to show that

(1)

there exists an outgoing transition labeled with
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
from
\vec{s}\mkern 2.0mu\vphantom{s}^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
, and

(2)

\xi^{\prime}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}})=m\cdot u^{\prime}
, with
u^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}^{*}
.

We know that

s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1}
and

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2}
,
so there exists a run
\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{2}
such that

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{2})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}=u\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
.
Because

\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha_{1}\cdot s_{2})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{S})){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}
and
\{\!\!\{B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\}\!\!\}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\in\mathcal{P}}
implements
\mathcal{S}
,
it follows that

u\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m\in\operatorname{pref}(\mathcal{L}(B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}))
.
Let
t\in Q_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
be the state reached on
u
in
B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
.
The state
t
is unique since
B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
is deterministic.
Because
u\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
is a prefix in
B_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
, there exists a transition

t\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m}t_{1}\in\delta_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
.
It holds that
(\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}=u
, so it follows that
\vec{s^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}=t
and there exists an outgoing transition from
\vec{s^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
labeled with
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
. This establishes
(1)
.

(2)
is established from the fact that send actions are immediately followed by their matching receive action in
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))
, and therefore all channels in
\xi
are empty, including
\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}})
.
Because
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
does not concern
\xi({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}})
,
m
remains the first unmatched send action from
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
to
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
in
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w
,
and thus
m
is at the head of channel
\xi^{\prime}({{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}})
:

(\vec{s_{0}},\xi_{0})\xrightarrow{\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}(\vec{s},\xi)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m\cdot w}(\vec{s^{\prime}},\xi^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m}(\vec{s^{\prime\prime}},\xi^{\prime\prime})\kern 5.0pt.

This concludes our proof of
1
.

Next, we argue that
I(v_{0})=\emptyset
. This claim follows trivially from the observation that every run in
I(v_{0})
must begin with
\alpha\cdot s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s^{\prime}
,
and therefore
v_{0}
must satisfy

v_{0}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}\leq u\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}?m
, yet

v_{0}{\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}}=u\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\triangleleft{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}?m
and we find a contradiction.

No Mixed Choice.

Assume that NMC does not hold for a pair of transitions

s_{1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m}s_{2},s\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m}s^{\prime}\in T
.
The negation of NMC says that
s_{1}
and
s
are simultaneously reachable.
Let
u\in Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}}^{*}
be a word such that

s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s_{1},s
.

Because
s_{0}\xRightarrow[{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}]{u}\mathrel{\vphantom{\to}{}^{*}}s
, there exists a run
\alpha\cdot s
such that
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s)){\Downarrow}_{Σ_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}}}=u
.

Let
\bar{w}
be
\texttt{split}(\texttt{trace}(\alpha\cdot s))
.
Let
\bar{w}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}!m\cdot{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}!m
be our witness
v_{0}
; we show that
v_{0}
satisfies
1
and
2
.

The reasoning is similar to that for the witness constructed for Send Coherence Condition, and is thus omitted.
∎

Appendix B
Additional Material for Section
5

See
5.9

Proof.

The arguments for co-NP membership of implementability for global types are identical to those for general finite protocols, and are thus omitted.

As in the proof of Theorem 5.8, we show NP-hardness of non-implementability via a reduction from the 3-SAT problem.
Assume a 3-SAT instance
\varphi=C_{1}\land\ldots\land C_{k}
. Let
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
be the variables occurring in
\varphi
and let
L_{ij}
be the
j
th literal of clause
C_{i}
, with
1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq 3
.

We construct a global type
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
over participants
\mathcal{P}=\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}},\dots,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{n}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{n}}}\}
, such that
\varphi
is satisfiable iff
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is implementable.
In particular, we ensure that
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is implementable iff
\mathsf{avail}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\}}(m,G^{\prime})
does not hold for some subterm
G^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
.

The construction idea for
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is identical to that for
\mathcal{S}_{\varphi}
from Theorem 5.8, but with several modifications to yield a tree-shaped protocol which corresponds to a global type.
First, for each branching state from which
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
selects variables or clauses, represented as
\mu t
terms, we introduce a new branch that acts as a forward edge connecting to the next branching state.
Because branches in a global type can only join at a single state via recursion variables, and recursion variables must appear in scope of their
\mu t
terms, variable and clause selection proceeds by recursing “backwards” towards the top-level global type. Due to this reversal of traversal order, the initial choice by
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
and the message exchange
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m
potentially violating Receive Coherence swap places in the protocol.
The construction of global type
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is detailed below:

(1)

Define for every variable
x_{i}
with
2<i<n
a global type
G_{x_{i}}
representing a truth assignment to variable
x_{i}
as follows:

\small G_{x_{i}}\coloneq\mu t_{x_{i}}.~+\;\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}:\bot.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{i}}}:\top.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m_{x_{i}}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}:m.\,t_{x_{i+1}}\,\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{i}}}:\bot.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}:\top.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m_{\overline{x}_{i}}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{i}}}:m.\,t_{x_{i+1}}\,\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}:next.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{i}}}:next.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:next.\,G_{x_{i-1}}\,\end{cases}

For
x_{2}
and
x_{n}
, the construction is modified as follows.
For
G_{x_{n}}
, the recursion variable in the first and second branches is replaced with
t_{C_{1}}
.
For
G_{x_{2}}
, the following is added before
G_{x_{1}}
in the third branch:

\small{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:\textit{last}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:\textit{last}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}:\textit{last}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}:\textit{last}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}:m.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}:m.\,

(2)

Define for every clause
C_{i}=L_{i1}\lor L_{i2}\lor L_{i3}
with
2\leq i<k
a global type
G_{C_{i}}
as follows, where
x_{ij}
is defined as
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x}}
if
L_{ij}=x
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}}}
if
L_{ij}=\neg x
:

\small G_{C_{i}}\coloneq\mu t_{C_{i}}.~+\;\begin{cases}\Sigma_{j=1..3}\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to x_{ij}:m.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m_{x_{ij}}.\,x_{ij}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m.\,t_{C_{i+1}}\,\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to x_{i1}:\textit{next}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to x_{i2}:\textit{next}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to x_{i3}:\textit{next}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:\textit{next}.\,G_{C_{i-1}}\,\end{cases}

For
C_{1}
and
C_{k}
, the construction is modified as follows.

For
G_{C_{1}}
, the last branch continues with
G_{x_{n}}
.
For
G_{C_{k}}
, the recursion variable in the first three branches is replaced with
t
.

(3)

Define
G_{x_{1}}
for variable
x_{1}
as follows:

\small G_{x_{1}}\coloneq+\;\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m_{1}.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m.\,\overline{G}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}:m_{2}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m.\,0\,\end{cases}\\
\overline{G}\coloneq+\;\begin{cases}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}:\bot.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}:\top.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m_{x_{1}}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}:m.\,t_{x_{2}}\,\\
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}:\bot.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}:\top.\,{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m_{\overline{x}_{1}}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}:m.\,t_{x_{2}}\,\end{cases}

The global type
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is thus defined as:

\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}\coloneq\mu t.~{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:\textit{top}.\,{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m.\,G_{C_{k}}

Observe that
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is linear in the size of
\varphi
.

We first establish that
\mathsf{avail}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\}}(m,\overline{G})
holds in
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
iff
\varphi
is satisfiable. Observe that the
G_{x_{i}}
’s contain two branches that recurse “backwards” to the previous
G_{x_{i+1}}
, and one branch that proceeds “forwards” towards
G_{x_{1}}
. Each time a backward branch is taken, either
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{i}}}
is added to the blocked set
\mathcal{B}
along the path. Forward branches do not change the blocked set, as participant
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
does not send messages in them. Thus, the path computed by
\mathsf{avail}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\}}(m,\overline{G})
from
\overline{G}
to
G_{C_{1}}
must contain for each variable
x_{i}
either
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{i}}}
.
The blocked set
\mathcal{B}
thus encodes a truth assignment
\rho_{\mathcal{B}}
for the
x_{i}
’s where
\rho_{\mathcal{B}}(x_{i})=\top
iff
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{i}}}\not\in\mathcal{B}
.
By construction of
G_{x_{i}}
, for every truth assignment
\rho
, there exists at least one path between
\overline{G}
and
G_{C_{1}}
such that
\rho=\rho_{\mathcal{B}}
for the blocked set
\mathcal{B}
computed along that path.

The
G_{C_{i}}
terms allow
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
to proceed backwards towards
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
by selecting a branch whose participant
x
is not in
\mathcal{B}
, i.e.
C_{i}
is satisfied by
\rho_{\mathcal{B}}
.
Thus, a path from
G_{C_{1}}
to
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
adds
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
to
\mathcal{B}
at
t_{i}
iff
\rho_{\mathcal{B}}
does not satisfy at least one of the clauses
C_{i}
.
Therefore,
m
is available in
\overline{G}
iff there exists a
\mathcal{B}
such that
\rho_{\mathcal{B}}
satisfies
\varphi
.

The reasoning that
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is implementable iff
\mathsf{avail}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\}}(m,\overline{G})
does not hold again follows that for
\mathcal{S}_{\varphi}
, and below we only discuss new behavior introduced by the structural changes to
\mathcal{S}_{\varphi}
.

Participant
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
still dictates the control flow in the global type, but now additionally sends
next
messages to inform participants in the branch when a forward edge is taken,
last
messages to inform
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}
when the last forward edge is taken, and
top
to
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
to inform
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
to receive
m
from
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
.
Receiving
next
messages means inaction for all other participants.
Receiving
last
prompts
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
to send a message to
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{x_{1}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{\overline{x}_{1}}}
, which they anticipate by receiving
last
first from
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}
.

As before, the only potential source of non-implementability lies in participant
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}
, who can violate Receive Coherence for transitions labeled with

{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{r}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m
and

{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}:m
in
G_{x_{1}}
when

\mathsf{avail}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\}}(m,\overline{G})
does not hold, and the message from
{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}}
can be received out of order.

We obtain that
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
is non-implementable iff
\mathsf{avail}_{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{p}}},{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}},\{{{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\mathtt{q}}}\}}(m,\overline{G})
holds in
\mathbf{G}_{\varphi}
iff
\varphi
is satisfiable.
∎
